{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — solver-trace-ablation\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (NLP and Symbolic Reasoning track)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026\\n\\n## Core Claims (3 条 — 诊断/分析论文)\\n\\n### Claim 1: ISR as Reward Hacking Diagnostic\\nSolver Trace Ablation (STA) derives ISR (Intensional Structural Reward) via component necessity testing (leave-one-out ablation on solver inputs). ISR detects reward hacking that prove_rate alone misses: exec-only GRPO achieves PR=93% but ISR collapses from 0.6423 (100 steps) to 0.1324 (500 steps), quantifying structural degeneration invisible to the execution reward.\\n\\n**证据**:\\n- plan003: 100-step checkpoint held-out eval PR=32%, ISR=0.6423 (proved-only) — early training produces structurally sound formalizations\\n- plan001-s42: 500-step checkpoint held-out eval PR=93%, ISR=0.1324, median_ISR=0.0 — late training achieves high prove_rate via structural shortcuts\\n- 3-seed consistency: s42 conclusion embedding (ISR=0.123), s43 repetition collapse (PR=13%, ISR=0), s44 tautological collapse (PR=98%, ISR=0.57 fake-high, n_comp=2.04)\\n- Hacking taxonomy: 5 distinct modes identified and quantified (plan012 `hacking_characterization_report.md` + plan017 D027)\\n\\n### Claim 2: GRPO Saturation Mechanism\\nExec-only GRPO exhibits a saturation failure mode: when prove_rate → 1.0, reward_std → 0 and loss → 0, training effectively stalls. Any reward component introducing variance (ISR, random noise) prevents saturation but does not prevent hacking — it redirects hacking into different structural failure modes.\\n\\n**证据**:\\n- plan001 exec-only: PR saturation → loss=0 → learning stops (D014)\\n- plan013 random reward: PR=1.0 (prevents saturation) but 100% P→P tautological collapse (D018)\\n- plan002 additive ISR β=0.3: PR=18%, ISR=0.1427 — ISR bonus prevents saturation but causes repetitive cycling, lowest PR of all conditions (D020)\\n- plan005 convex combination: penalty effect confirmed — any ISR<1 reduces reward (D010)\\n- plan017 additive random β=0.3: eval PR=48%, ISR=0.045, comp=9.56 — \\\"complexity-without-utility\\\" mode (高 component 但 conclusion 不依赖 premises, 33% proved ISR=0). ISR 比 random 更差 (PR 18% vs 48%), ISR 信号对训练 actively misleading (D026/D027)\\n- ⚠️ **措辞约束 (Reviewer D027)**: plan013 (0.9·r_exec+0.1·random) vs plan017 (r_exec·(1+0.3·random)) 公式结构不同，不可简化为\\\"additive vs convex\\\"对照。plan017 vs plan002 的 ISR vs random 对比是纯净的（唯一变量是 random vs ISR）\\n\\n### Claim 3: Cross-Logic Generality (conditional on plan006)\\nSTA protocol is solver-agnostic: the 5-step derivation (pilot → collect shortcuts → classify → map RIP → design check) transfers to structurally different formal languages. Hacking modes and ISR diagnostic value replicate across FOL→Prover9 and Prolog→SWI-Prolog.\\n\\n**证据**: 待验证。plan006 Prolog GRPO 实验未启动。如果 plan006 无法复现 hacking + ISR 诊断，此 claim 降级为 future work。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: GRPO with execution-only rewards trains neurosymbolic formalizers that achieve high solve rates but produce structurally degenerate outputs (reward hacking). Current practice evaluates only prove_rate, missing structural quality entirely. No principled diagnostic exists for detecting and characterizing this failure mode.\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Mellgren et al. (2025) hand-craft Prolog-specific structure penalties. EVOM (2026) uses scalar execution rewards only. ExecVerify/EGCA derive trace-level rewards for code, not formal logic. No work systematically studies reward hacking in formal reasoning GRPO or provides diagnostic tools for it.\\n- **Our contribution**: We propose STA (Solver Trace Ablation), applying component necessity testing (LOO ablation) to derive ISR as a **diagnostic metric** for structural integrity. Key findings:\\n  1. ISR quantifies hacking invisible to prove_rate (64% → 13% collapse over training)\\n  2. GRPO saturation creates a \\\"training trap\\\": PR→1 means no gradient signal, but adding any variance (ISR or random) redirects rather than prevents hacking\\n  3. Different reward formulations produce distinct, classifiable hacking strategies (5 modes identified)\\n  4. ISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness — this boundary is important for practitioners\\n- **Actionable insights for practitioners**:\\n  1. Never trust prove_rate alone — use ISR + component count as joint diagnostic\\n  2. Single-seed GRPO results are unreliable (2/3 exec-only seeds collapse differently)\\n  3. Adding structural reward components can hurt prove_rate (penalty effect) or redirect hacking (saturation effect) — neither naïvely improves quality\\n  4. ISR is a structural diagnostic, not a semantic quality proxy\\n- **Headline result**: \\\"We demonstrate that GRPO-trained FOL formalizers achieving 93-98% prove rate produce structurally degenerate outputs detectable by ISR — a component necessity metric derived from solver trace ablation. Across 5 reward conditions and 3 seeds, we identify 5 distinct hacking strategies and a GRPO saturation mechanism that confounds all reward-augmentati\\n\\n... PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. ```text
Now I have the full picture. Let me analyze the framing in light of plan018 results.

The current paper_framing.md says:

**Claim 2** includes: "plan002 additive ISR β=0.3: PR=18%, ISR=0.1427 — ISR bonus prevents saturation but causes repetitive cycling, lowest PR of all conditions (D020)"

And: "plan017 additive random β=0.3: eval PR=48%, ISR=0.045 — ISR 比 random 更差 (PR 18% vs 48%), ISR 信号对训练 actively misleading (D026/D027)"


But the new results show something different — at β=0.1, plan002-b01 achieves PR=0.81 with ISR=0.3365, while plan018 random gets PR=0.79 with ISR=0.1038, meaning ISR produces 3.2x higher structure than random at matched performance. This reveals the "ISR harmful" conclusion was actually β-dependent, holding at β=0.3 but not at lower values where ISR genuinely improves training dynamics.

The framing hasn't incorporated these latest experiments yet, still claiming ISR-as-reward failed completely, when plan002-b01 and plan018 demonstrate it works effectively at low dose. Rather than a problem, this is actually a positive finding that opens two paths forward: either add a nuance about dose-dependent effects to the current diagnostic framing, or pivot back to ISR-as-reward with a focus on the dose dependency.

Now I'm verifying the technical compliance across spec integrity, eval methodology, data handling, and GPU allocation—all checks pass, and the core finding holds: ISR at β=0.1 substantially outperforms random at the same dose on structural metrics while maintaining comparable prove rates. Ready to write up the review.
```

```text
Confirmed: plan002-b01 also used Qwen3ForCausalLM (Qwen3-4B). Both experiments used the same model. The registry's `model_name: Mistral-Small-3.1-24B-Instruct-2503` for plan018 is definitively a metadata error.

Now let me also verify the GPU boundary (L1 #4). From the plan018 spec:
- gpu: ["cuda:0"]
- The constraint file doesn't explicitly list gpu_ids, but the project uses 2 × RTX PRO 6000 (cuda:0, cuda:1), and C002/C005 require explicit CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES. 

The spec shows plan018 ran on cuda:0, which is within bounds. However, the config file `plan018_additive_random_b01_s42.yaml` referenced in the registry command doesn't exist on the server—it may have been a temporary file that was deleted or overwritten after training completed.

Looking at the existing `grpo_additive_random_b01.yaml` config, it points to a different output directory than what plan018 actually used, so either a separate config was created and later cleaned up, or the command used CLI overrides to change the output path. Since the experiment ran successfully with the correct model and parameters, this appears to be a cleanup issue rather than a functional problem.

Now I'm compiling my full review. The main issue I've found is a registry mismatch: plan018's registry entry lists Mistral-24B as the model, but the actual checkpoint is Qwen3-4B.

I also notice the config file that plan018 references doesn't exist on the server, which creates a reproducibility gap. The eval results do match the registry metrics though, so the data itself is consistent. Plan002-b01's spec is incomplete—it's missing model_name, seed, and reward_formula details, which is a pre-existing issue.

The Agent's core argument holds up: at β=0.1, ISR shows genuine structural guidance rather than random noise. The performance ratio is nearly identical (0.81 vs 0.79), but ISR achieves 3.2x better improvement (0.337 vs 0.104) with the same number of components, which is the key evidence supporting their conclusion.

Now I'm seeing that proof compactness also favors ISR—8.58 vs 8.46 components—which is actually a reversal from the β=0.3 case where ISR performed worse than random. The pattern emerging is dose-dependent: at higher regularization (β=0.3), ISR becomes harmful, but at lower regularization (β=0.1), it's beneficial. Let me cross-check whether the eval data from plan002-b01 and plan018 align.
```

```text
plan002-b01 eval results match the registry:
- prove_rate: 0.81 ✓
- mean_isr: 0.3365 ✓
- isr_std: 0.3227 ✓
- proved_only_mean_isr: 0.4154 ✓
- n_proved: 81 ✓

Now let me also check that both evals used the same test data. plan018's eval summary says `solver: Prover9Solver, logic_type: fol, model_path: /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan018_additive_random_b01_s42`. 

Looking at plan002-b01's registry, the eval_data is `proofwriter_fol_100.jsonl`. From plan018's eval_results.json, I can see it also used Prover9Solver with fol logic type, and the first test item is folio_765. Both evaluations appear to be using the same test set, so I can now compile the review.

The data consistency checks out — both eval outputs match their registry metrics exactly, both use proofwriter_fol_100.jsonl with 100 samples in FOL format with Prover9Solver, and both are running the Qwen3-4B model. The agent's comparison numbers all check out: PR is effectively tied at 0.81 vs 0.79, ISR shows a 3.2x difference at 0.337 vs 0.104, component counts are 5.58 vs 8.46, and proved-only ISR is 0.415 vs 0.131. However, there's a discrepancy I need to flag — plan018's registry lists the model_name as Mistral-Small-3.1, which doesn't match the Qwen3-4B that's actually being used.

Beyond that, there are a few other issues: the config file referenced for plan018 doesn't exist on the server, which creates a reproducibility gap; plan002-b01-s42 has an incomplete spec missing key details like model_name and seed; and there's a minor naming inconsistency in the eval output files between the two plans that could cause issues downstream.

The core finding holds up well though — the matched control design is solid, and the fact that PR stays nearly identical (0.81 vs 0.79) while ISR differs by 3.2x, combined with the component count difference (5.58 vs 8.46), provides strong evidence that ISR is genuinely guiding structure rather than just regularizing variance. The "negative" tag in the notes is a bit confusing since it refers to plan018's hypothesis being rejected, not the overall finding being negative for the project.

I'm noticing the Agent attributed this to "Claim 2" about structural value at low ISR doses, but the experiment plan maps plan018 to Claim 1 about ISR as a diagnostic signal. The paper_framing likely got updated in D028, so the claim numbering might have shifted. The data analysis itself is sound regardless of which claim number it ultimately supports.

The dose-dependent pattern is genuinely compelling — ISR at β=0.1 matches random baseline on PR but dramatically outperforms on ISR metrics, while β=0.3 fails entirely. This isn't rationalization or cherry-picking; both conditions are reported honestly and the conclusion about low-dose structural value is well-calibrated to the evidence. I'll move forward with the review.

eval PR | 0.93 | 0.96 | +3.2% |\\\\n| eval ISR | 0.123 | 0.0027 | -97.8% |\\\\n| proved_only ISR | 0.132 | 0.0028 | -97.9% |\\\\n| components | 6.58 | 18.55 | +182% |\\\\n| ISR=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n| n_necessary=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n\\\\n## Key findings:\\\\n1. PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Model generates longer proofs with more components, but 94.8% have ZERO necessary components.\\\\n3. NOT tautological collapse (comp=2 ratio=0). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. Windowed ISR trend is monotonically decreasing: 0.1115 (101-200) → 0.0433 (401-500) → 0.0200 (601-700) → 0.0144 (901-1000). No recovery signal.\\\\n5. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. 1	# Plan006 Prolog GRPO Readiness Report

3	**Date**: 2026-05-14

6	## 检查结果
7	
8	### 1. Prolog 数据
9	- **不需要单独的 Prolog 数据文件**。训练数据仍使用 `data/train_500_seed42.jsonl`（500 条，C001 compliant）
10	- `build_prolog_dataset()` 使用相同 JSONL 但切换 prompt 为 Prolog 格式（`PROLOG_SYSTEM_PROMPT`），让模型将 NL 形式化为 Prolog 而非 FOL
11	- 实验设计正确：相同 NL 输入，不同形式化目标
12	
13	### 2. 训练代码
14	- `train_grpo.py` **完全支持** Prolog：
15	  - `--logic_type prolog` + `--solver prolog` CLI 参数已实现
16	  - `build_prolog_dataset()` 使用 `PROLOG_SYSTEM_PROMPT` / `PROLOG_USER_TEMPLATE`
17	  - `make_reward_fn()` 在 `logic_type == "prolog"` 时自动切换到 `prolog_sta_reward` / `fast_prolog_sta_reward`
18	- Reward 代码：`src/sta/prolog_reward.py` 完整实现（parse、ablation、ISR 计算）
19	- Solver 代码：`src/solvers/prolog.py` 完整实现（`PrologSolver` via swipl subprocess）

23	- 简单证明测试：PASSED ✅
24	- `sta_prolog.py` 测试套件：**5/5 PASSED** ✅
25	
26	### 4. Config

30	  - `reward_mode: execution_only`, `exec_weight: 1.0`, `sta_weight: 0.0`
31	  - `output_dir: /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/grpo_prolog_exec_only`（C003 ✅）

47	```bash
48	# Seed 42 (GPU N)
49	export CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=N
50	cd /root/solver-trace-ablation && source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base
51	nohup python scripts/train_grpo.py \
52	  --config configs/grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml \
53	  --seed 42 \
54	  --output_dir /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan006_prolog_exec_s42 \

Config\n- `configs/grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml` 已存在，配置正确：\n  - `data_path: data/train_500_seed42.jsonl`（C001 ✅）\n  - `logic_type: prolog`, `solver: prolog`\n  - `reward_mode: execution_only`, `exec_weight: 1.0`, `sta_weight: 0.0`\n  - `output_dir: /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/grpo_prolog_exec_only`（C003 ✅）\n  - `save_total_limit=1` hardcoded in GRPOConfig（C003 ✅）\n  - `seed: 42`\n\n### 5. Looking at the footnote in the evidence document, it claims the eval fields are consistent with eval_sta.py output format, but when I check the registry, plan002-additive-b03-s42 shows training metrics like overall_prove_rate=0.34025 and overall_isr=0.11527—these are clearly training results, not evaluation results. There's a discrepancy here between what the evidence document reports and what's actually in the registry.

The Table 1 footnote directly states that plan002-b03's eval comes from paper_framing, not from eval_sta.py, which contradicts the evidence document's attribution. I need to trace where the PR=0.18 value actually originates by checking paper_framing, and then reconcile which source is correct for the ISR values as well.

Looking at the component count issue, comp=6.58 is supposedly cross-referenced from plan001-exec-only-s44 notes, but that's problematic since s42 and s44 use different seeds and shouldn't share metrics. I should verify the actual value in the local eval_sta_plan001_exec_only_s42.json file to confirm what's really stored.

The most pressing problems are that the 3-seed mean appears to mix different evaluation methods, and the provenance of plan002-b03's metrics remains unclear.
